The daily field guide for digital media

AI Media Scout

2026-10-30 · All platforms · Open-source software

Today’s expanded edition offers 49 detailed guides, including 20 completed from earlier screened entries, and 31 additional newly screened tools. It adds 60 distinct tools to the cumulative library. Coverage spans 75 fields, 623 configured repository queries and 18 model tasks; archived search failures, partial results and bounded pages remain disclosed below. Live primary-source research supplements every field, including neural audio patching and learned light shaping. All platforms were searched equally; individual tools retain their documented platform limits. The two new GitLab guides distinguish software grants from model/service costs. None of these applications was installed or run by AI Media Scout, and no new entry earned Recommended or Ready to try without independent evidence. First profile means new to this library, not necessarily launched today. All repository queries returned successful attempts, with 122 bounded searches. One text-to-image model-task response exceeded the collector’s 2 MB archival limit; web research supplements that explicit gap. Codeberg and SourceHut access gaps are recorded separately.

Search and review counts

75 creative fields; 623/623 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 28237 distinct source candidates and 810 model leads. 49 detailed profiles and 31 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 122 bounded repository queries, 1 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

Today’s field coverage

FieldFinding
Images & designPrimary image-canvas documentation was researched, including Invoke and Moka. Browser/native interfaces do not establish local inference support; model and hardware terms remain separate. Moka’s project page had no extractable text in this web read. Source 1 Source 2
Video, animation & filmOpenReelio’s primary site describes an inspectable AI command log with a multitrack editor. New editing workspaces were searched broadly; polished interface claims do not establish reliable automated edits. Source 1
Audio, music & voiceLocal neural music studios and synthesis were researched. OpenSuno’s primary site distinguishes MIT application code from non-commercial YuE2 and associated weights; Windows support remains experimental. Source 1
3D, reconstruction & assetsParametric modeling and AI-agent CAD were researched through SolidCraft and SOMA. Editable geometry/export capability is distinct from verified dimensional accuracy or fabrication readiness. Source 1 Source 2
AI audio patching and musical dataflowThe expanded search found a learned Max for Live performance mixer and an agent-driven OSC/MIDI integration. Both are screened leads with specific setup/documentation discrepancies, rather than qualified performance recommendations. RAVE and nn_tilde remain excluded because their reviewed software grants are non-commercial. The primary RAVE paper was revisited for learned audio synthesis. IRCAM’s project page returned mainly navigation in the text view; neither that partial view nor the paper changes the reviewed non-commercial software grant. Source 1 Source 2 Source 3 Source 4 Source 5 Source 6
Learned light shaping and caustic artLearned caustic-image generation and neural shape-from-caustics research were examined as light-art adjacencies. The web research did not establish a newly eligible, licensed creator application. Classical caustic optimization alone is not evidence of a neural tool. Source 1 Source 2
Browser tools & web mediaBrowser AI and creative-canvas sources were researched. WebLLM provides an in-browser model runtime, whereas ordinary WebGPU effects are not automatically AI tools. Source 1
WebXR, VR & ARMeta’s primary IWSDK announcement describes agent-written and tested immersive-web scenes. This establishes an AI-assisted authoring route, not universal headset support or independently verified experience quality. Source 1
Computational art & creative codingCreative-coding environments and neural/agent authoring were researched. Fragment’s ordinary shader and MIDI tools are not counted as AI simply because an agent could write code for them. Source 1
Interactive, immersive & live mediaPrimary installation software documentation includes ONNX pose detection controlling media and lights. Interaction support remains distinct from reliable unattended operation. Source 1
3D printing & generative CADSOMA’s primary documentation covers AI-authored editable CadQuery models and physical-design examples. A STEP/STL export is not a guarantee of printability, load capacity or manufactured accuracy. Source 1
Games & production pipelinesForgeAX and Evoke broadened the game search from agent-authored editable code to learned interactive worlds. Their architecture and author demos do not establish universal game-production readiness. Source 1
Motion capture & character animationMovens-Zero’s primary project documents recovered and generated motion from several modalities. Research/model availability alone does not establish an artist-ready animation application. Source 1
Avatars, digital humans & lip syncAVTR-1’s primary model card links the inference code and specifies CUDA/TensorRT and Ampere-or-newer GPU prerequisites. Weight availability and an avatar demo do not settle all software/asset terms. Source 1
VFX, compositing & relightingLight-aware compositing and BlenderFusion research supplemented keying/compositing discovery. A paper or conventional compositor alone is not a newly reviewed open-source AI application. Source 1
Spatial audio & volumetric mediaNeural acoustic-field research was examined for immersive spatial sound. Conventional room simulation is not classified as neural inference without concrete evidence. Source 1
Photogrammetry, scanning & neural renderingPrimary volumetric-capture research describes RGB/RGB-D reconstruction into point clouds and Gaussian splats. Capture quality, synchronization and usable source licensing require separate workflow checks. Source 1
Editing, captions & post-productionSubtitle Edit’s current primary documentation lists local/cloud speech models and AI review alongside manual subtitle tools. Recognition, translation, timing and provider privacy remain separate checks. Source 1
Vector graphics, illustration & texturesSVGRender and StarVector primary sources cover learned raster-to-vector and text/image-to-SVG workflows. Editable output is not proof of geometric cleanliness or design quality. Source 1
Typography, fonts & layoutVecGlypher research generates editable vector glyphs with image/text conditioning. Source availability, font rights, script coverage and operator setup were not established merely from the paper. Source 1
Storyboarding, narrative & comicsPrimary storyboard/comic project sites were researched. Dramai’s own page says its v0.1 ships storyboard generation while later pipeline stages remain planned, so marketing-stage diagrams are not treated as completed functionality. Source 1

Dralo Studio

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · Apache-2.0 · recently-created

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Developing a short film or motion comic from a script while keeping local project data, assets and shot revisions together.

Evidence checked 2026-10-30; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Why Experimental: The current R3 standalone source release is explicitly a beta; model and platform validation is incomplete, and older database migrations have restricted upgrade paths. [1] [3] [4]
  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]

Create the virtual environment, install the locked Python dependencies, initialize local configuration, install both frontend dependency trees, build, then initialize the database and run the API and worker in separate terminals. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1] [3] [4]

Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [1] [3] [4]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. R3 is a standalone source beta. Old or unknown databases require the published upgrade procedure, not direct replacement. Local storage does not mean model inference stays offline; some providers need publicly reachable reference-asset storage.

How it uses AI

A standalone short-drama workspace connects AI-assisted scripts, reusable characters, storyboards, image/video generation and a multitrack timeline through user-configured model providers. [1] [3] [4]

Introduction

A standalone short-drama workspace connects AI-assisted scripts, reusable characters, storyboards, image/video generation and a multitrack timeline through user-configured model providers. [1] [3] [4]

What it is good for

Developing a short film or motion comic from a script while keeping local project data, assets and shot revisions together. [1] [3] [4]

Demo & examples

The README explicitly labels its workflow graphics as diagrams rather than screenshots or tested generated films. They illustrate organization, not output-quality evidence. [1] [3] [4]

Install

Prepare Node 22+, pnpm 11.x, Python 3.12 and FFmpeg/FFprobe. Create the virtual environment, install the locked Python dependencies, initialize local configuration, install both frontend dependency trees, build, then initialize the database and run the API and worker in separate terminals. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare Node 22+, pnpm 11.x, Python 3.12 and FFmpeg/FFprobe. Create the virtual environment, install the locked Python dependencies, initialize local configuration, install both frontend dependency trees, build, then initialize the database and run the API and worker in separate terminals.
python -m venv .venv
python -m pip install --only-binary=:all: -r apps/server/requirements.lock.txt
python scripts/init_local_config.py
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile
pnpm --dir apps/director-upstream install --frozen-lockfile
pnpm build
cd apps/server
python -m app.standalone_initialize
python -m uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000
python -m app.jobs.worker

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4]

  1. Activate the same Python environment separately for the API and worker, then open the local page and sign in using the locally initialized account.
  2. Add your own model channel, import a short script and organize characters and shots.
  3. Generate one reviewed shot before assembling media and subtitles on the timeline; inspect output and provider usage.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node.js 22+, repository-pinned pnpm 11.x, Python 3.12, FastAPI/SQLite and FFmpeg/FFprobe. The source guide separately installs the 3D director’s frontend dependencies.
Platforms
The first public source install was checked on Windows/Python 3.12.14. macOS/Linux instructions exist but do not establish completed platform validation. No standalone Windows/macOS installer is supplied.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4]

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 original code; integrated MIT components retain notices. Model providers, cloud object storage, user-supplied character assets and generation costs have independent terms. No model weights, provider credits or unlicensed character packs are included.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 original code; integrated MIT components retain notices. Model providers, cloud object storage, user-supplied character assets and generation costs have independent terms. No model weights, provider credits or unlicensed character packs are included.
  • Cost: Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4]

Limitations

R3 is a standalone source beta. Old or unknown databases require the published upgrade procedure, not direct replacement. Local storage does not mean model inference stays offline; some providers need publicly reachable reference-asset storage. [1] [3] [4]

Metroidvania Studio

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Drafting connected rooms, autotiled maps and minimaps for later integration with Unity, Godot, Unreal or SDL projects.

Evidence checked 2026-10-30; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]

For agent control, install the CLI and configure a compatible MCP client to launch a version-pinned server against an existing workspace. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1] [3]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Layout files do not supply your game’s gameplay. Browser editing and offline MCP writers must not modify the same workspace concurrently. The MCP server itself makes no external requests, but an external AI client may do so.

How it uses AI

A room-based 2D level editor exposes inspection, Lua generation, dry-run edits, previews and JSON export through a local MCP server so an AI agent can help build editable game layouts. [1] [3]

Introduction

A room-based 2D level editor exposes inspection, Lua generation, dry-run edits, previews and JSON export through a local MCP server so an AI agent can help build editable game layouts. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Drafting connected rooms, autotiled maps and minimaps for later integration with Unity, Godot, Unreal or SDL projects. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The README provides editing GIFs and a six-room sample world. These demonstrate editor operations, not independently validated AI level-design quality. [1] [3]

Install

Use a release launcher with ASP.NET Core Runtime 10, or build with Node 24+, .NET SDK 10 and Git. For agent control, install the CLI and configure a compatible MCP client to launch a version-pinned server against an existing workspace. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use a release launcher with ASP.NET Core Runtime 10, or build with Node 24+, .NET SDK 10 and Git. For agent control, install the CLI and configure a compatible MCP client to launch a version-pinned server against an existing workspace.
npm install --global metroidvania-studio
metroidvania-studio mcp --workspace workspace

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Open the sample world and inspect its room connections before making changes.
  2. Have the AI client inspect capabilities and the current revision, propose a dry-run batch or Lua edit, and review the layout.
  3. Apply the reviewed revision, preview and validate it, then export the room JSON and integrate the relevant engine package.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node.js 24+, .NET 10 runtime for CLI/MCP; .NET SDK 10 and Git for source builds. The MCP transport is local stdio; connected editor mode uses an explicitly selected loopback server.
Platforms
Windows, macOS and Linux launch/build routes are documented. The Windows program and local-browser editor have distinct packages. No game engine is required to edit maps.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: MIT project-owned code/assets; engine integrations and other dependencies retain their notices. No inference model is bundled. The chosen AI client/model has separate terms and may send map inputs/results to its provider.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT project-owned code/assets; engine integrations and other dependencies retain their notices. No inference model is bundled. The chosen AI client/model has separate terms and may send map inputs/results to its provider.
  • Cost: Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

Soundbite

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · MIT · recently-created

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

Docs checked 2026-10-30 · 1 GitHub stars · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Turn selected passages from podcasts, voice notes and recorded discussions into captioned vertical, square or widescreen clips without uploading the recording for transcription.

Evidence checked 2026-10-30; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The README links a hosted editor and an editor screenshot. Its 10–45 seconds per two-minute transcription claim is an author report, not our benchmark or an independent quality test. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Use the hosted editor without installation, or obtain the official source, install its npm dependencies and run the demo at http://localhost:4340/demo/. Build the embeddable distribution separately with npm run build. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: The latest-release query returned no records. The latest-updated query returned 0 issue/pull-request records, with a maximum of 10. This snapshot and the reviewed setup/limitations do not establish sustained support or all blocker resolutions. [3] [4]
  • Independent use · unknown: No substantive independent first-hand use was established in the reviewed primary documentation and bounded issue activity. Owner reports, PR descriptions and promotional comments do not independently validate this workflow. [4] [1]
  • Models, services and costs · documented: Whisper tiny multilingual is downloaded from Hugging Face. App MIT licensing does not replace model, font, icon, stock-media or optional service terms. MIT application; local computation. The hosted high-resolution route uses rewarded ads, and optional media providers have separate terms. [1]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The README’s broad no-upload claim applies to core local processing; model downloads, optional stock searches and ads use networks. Long files and noisy speech can still produce mistakes. Hosted 1080p/1440p exports require rewarded ads; self-hosted copies without an ad unit do not. Minimum RAM/VRAM and total working storage are not stated.

How it uses AI

A browser audiogram editor uses multilingual Whisper through Transformers.js to transcribe and word-time recordings, then combines editable captions with designed video layouts. [1]

Introduction

A browser audiogram editor uses multilingual Whisper through Transformers.js to transcribe and word-time recordings, then combines editable captions with designed video layouts. [1]

What it is good for

Turn selected passages from podcasts, voice notes and recorded discussions into captioned vertical, square or widescreen clips without uploading the recording for transcription. [1]

Demo & examples

The README links a hosted editor and an editor screenshot. Its 10–45 seconds per two-minute transcription claim is an author report, not our benchmark or an independent quality test. [1]

Install

Use the hosted editor without installation, or obtain the official source, install its npm dependencies and run the demo at http://localhost:4340/demo/. Build the embeddable distribution separately with npm run build. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use the hosted editor without installation, or obtain the official source, install its npm dependencies and run the demo at http://localhost:4340/demo/. Build the embeddable distribution separately with npm run build.
git clone https://github.com/drkxbt/soundbite
cd soundbite
npm install
npm run demo

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Open a recording and select the passage to transcribe first; inspect language and every caption.
  2. Trim, split or reorder passages, correct word timing, choose a layout and add your own artwork.
  3. Export a short MP4 and inspect caption timing, joins and audio before exporting the complete clip.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
First use downloads roughly 44–66 MB of speech weights and 27 MB of ONNX WASM runtime. No dedicated GPU is required by the documented fallback; memory and disk minima are unspecified.
Software
A current browser with WebAssembly; WebGPU accelerates the encoder when available. WebCodecs accelerates export when supported. Node.js/npm are needed for the source route; a minimum Node version is not stated.
Platforms
Browser workflow; no independently checked OS/browser compatibility matrix. CPU/WASM fallback is documented, and browser feature support matters more than assuming support from the interface.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [5]

  • Weights: NOTICE identifies Whisper as MIT, downloaded as ONNX separately; runtime/fonts/drawings/stock media retain their stated MIT, Apache, OFL, CC or provider terms. Downloaded third-party assets are not all covered by the app’s MIT grant.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Whisper tiny multilingual is downloaded from Hugging Face. App MIT licensing does not replace model, font, icon, stock-media or optional service terms.
  • Cost: MIT application; local computation. The hosted high-resolution route uses rewarded ads, and optional media providers have separate terms.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

The README’s broad no-upload claim applies to core local processing; model downloads, optional stock searches and ads use networks. Long files and noisy speech can still produce mistakes. Hosted 1080p/1440p exports require rewarded ads; self-hosted copies without an ad unit do not. Minimum RAM/VRAM and total working storage are not stated. [1]

A CLI and built-in MCP server let an AI coding agent create and edit generative-art sketches, then render, sweep parameters and export images, HTML or video. [1]

What it is good for

Reproducible p5, Canvas2D, Three.js, GLSL and SVG sketch exploration with saved seeds, parameter sweeps and contact-sheet montages. [1]

Demo & examples

The README supplies a complete hybrid agent/CLI example. It is a workflow example rather than a viewed independent creative-output demonstration. [1]

Install

Install the official npm package globally. Diagnose the client configuration and browser/render dependencies before connecting an agent. Preview agent configuration changes with the documented dry-run option. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the official npm package globally. Diagnose the client configuration and browser/render dependencies before connecting an agent. Preview agent configuration changes with the documented dry-run option.
npm install -g @genart-dev/cli
genart agent doctor
genart agent install vscode --dry-run

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Create or import a sketch, then connect a separately configured agent for source edits.
  2. Validate and render one seed; vary parameters or seeds in a small batch and retain the manifest.
  3. Export HTML or images; use ffmpeg for video and sharp for a montage, then inspect the result.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Node.js/npm, Chrome/Chromium for rendering, ffmpeg on PATH for video, optional sharp for montage. Minimum Node version is not stated in this README.
Platforms
Command-line/browser-rendering workflow with macOS client paths illustrated. A complete tested OS matrix is not documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: The CLI includes no generative model; agent intelligence comes from the user’s chosen model/client. Renderer, browser and optional package licenses remain separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. The CLI includes no generative model; agent intelligence comes from the user’s chosen model/client. Renderer, browser and optional package licenses remain separate.
  • Cost: MIT CLI; local rendering plus the chosen agent/model costs.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Most batch/render operations are deterministic tools. AI relevance is the documented MCP sketch-authoring integration. Live or expensive sketches can hit rendering timeouts; seed reproducibility does not guarantee artistic quality. [1]

How it uses AI

A CLI/MCP tool gives an AI agent labelled video frames, motion tracks, contact sheets and assertions for inspecting and revising animations. [1]

Introduction

A CLI/MCP tool gives an AI agent labelled video frames, motion tracks, contact sheets and assertions for inspecting and revising animations. [1]

What it is good for

Grounding an agent’s animation feedback in frame addresses, motion measurements, onion-skin views and before/after comparisons. [1]

Demo & examples

The README shows a bouncing-ball clip, scan sheet, onion view and difference maps. It also reports synthetic/browser evaluation tasks; those author tests are not an independent first-hand workflow review. [1]

Install

Obtain the official repository, install dependencies with Bun and run its CLI on Node 24. ffmpeg is required. Configure the MCP server using the absolute path to the repository’s CLI entry. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Obtain the official repository, install dependencies with Bun and run its CLI on Node 24. ffmpeg is required. Configure the MCP server using the absolute path to the repository’s CLI entry.
git clone https://github.com/tylergibbs1/kymo
cd kymo
bun install
bun run --silent kymo -- probe clip.mp4

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Probe an existing clip and scan it with a small frame budget.
  2. Ask the agent to inspect labelled frames, track a selected region and compare the relevant timestamps.
  3. Validate a concrete assertion and inspect frames yourself; keep synthetic ground truth separate from real-world footage measurements.

How it uses AI

Install

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare Node 20+, Python 3.11/3.12 with uv, local Postgres and espeak-ng. Create the local database, run the FastAPI service, then install/start the Next.js frontend in a second terminal.
createdb text_to_audiobook
cd services/api && uv run uvicorn app.main:app --reload --port 8000
cd apps/web && npm install && npm run dev

Scope: Voicing permitted comic pages while manually reviewing OCR, reading order, speakers and individual takes rather than treating page analysis as final.

  • Creative results · documented: The English demo documents three panels and four manually annotated utterances with a local synthetic voice and sample-based timeline. This is author evidence, not a real-manga accuracy benchmark or independent evaluation. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Use the documented Windows source route with pinned Node/pnpm/Python versions, uv and Docker Desktop’s Linux engine. The setup script creates local configuration; download chosen models, start services and bootstrap the first owner. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: The latest-release query returned no records. The latest-updated query returned 0 issue/pull-request records, with a maximum of 10. This snapshot and the reviewed setup/limitations do not establish sustained support or all blocker resolutions. [3] [4]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. No automatic translation is included. English text/speech is the new-project default, reading direction is separate, and existing language settings persist. Manual editing needs no loaded GPU model. CI uses no GPU and does not prove recognition/speech quality.

How it uses AI

A local comic editor proposes page regions and character references with Qwen3-VL/SigLIP2, synthesizes reusable voices with VoxCPM2 and assembles timed spoken chapters. [1]

Introduction

A local comic editor proposes page regions and character references with Qwen3-VL/SigLIP2, synthesizes reusable voices with VoxCPM2 and assembles timed spoken chapters. [1]

What it is good for

Voicing permitted comic pages while manually reviewing OCR, reading order, speakers and individual takes rather than treating page analysis as final. [1]

Demo & examples

The English demo documents three panels and four manually annotated utterances with a local synthetic voice and sample-based timeline. This is author evidence, not a real-manga accuracy benchmark or independent evaluation. [1]

Install

Use the documented Windows source route with pinned Node/pnpm/Python versions, uv and Docker Desktop’s Linux engine. The setup script creates local configuration; download chosen models, start services and bootstrap the first owner. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use the documented Windows source route with pinned Node/pnpm/Python versions, uv and Docker Desktop’s Linux engine. The setup script creates local configuration; download chosen models, start services and bootstrap the first owner.
git clone https://github.com/Yurichz/manga-voice-studio.git
Set-Location manga-voice-studio
.\scripts\setup.ps1
.\scripts\download-models.ps1
.\studio.cmd start
pnpm bootstrap

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Import permitted pages and confirm regions, text, reading order and speakers.
  2. Design or import a permitted voice, listen and save its immutable version, then assign voices.
  3. Generate reviewed utterances, assemble the chapter and inspect synchronized playback before WAV/ZIP or separate MP3/JSON publication export.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Developer tested RTX 4090 with 24 GB VRAM; this is not a minimum. Current model weights total about 15.34 GB, with environments, cache and media requiring additional unspecified storage.
Software
Tested versions: Node 26.3.1, pnpm 11.25.0, Python 3.12.13 via uv; Docker Desktop Linux engine/WSL2, Postgres, Redis and the documented Python inference environment.
Platforms
Developer-verified Windows/RTX 4090 configuration. Other operating systems/GPUs are explicitly not verified, rather than assumed unsupported.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Original code MIT; Qwen3-VL, VoxCPM2, SigLIP2, databases and other components retain their own terms. Pages and voice recordings need their own permissions.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. Original code MIT; Qwen3-VL, VoxCPM2, SigLIP2, databases and other components retain their own terms. Pages and voice recordings need their own permissions.
  • Cost: MIT app and local inference/storage; separate dependency/model licenses apply.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

No automatic translation is included. English text/speech is the new-project default, reading direction is separate, and existing language settings persist. Manual editing needs no loaded GPU model. CI uses no GPU and does not prove recognition/speech quality. [1]

Software

How it uses AI

An MCP/CLI layer lets an AI assistant write build123d part scripts, inspect multiview renders and run FDM-oriented geometry checks before exporting a slicer file. [1]

Introduction

An MCP/CLI layer lets an AI assistant write build123d part scripts, inspect multiview renders and run FDM-oriented geometry checks before exporting a slicer file. [1]

What it is good for

Measured brackets, enclosures, spacers and simple remixes with named dimensions and small physical fit coupons. [1]

Demo & examples

The author shows printed enclosure examples and a bracket-remix preview that is explicitly not printed yet. A documented thin-wall failure illustrates why rendered appearance is not sufficient fit/print evidence. [1]

Install

Install uv and configure the documented b123d-print-mcp command in a compatible client, with a chosen parts workspace. Restart the client, confirm tool availability and set printer/material/calibration parameters. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install uv and configure the documented b123d-print-mcp command in a compatible client, with a chosen parts workspace. Restart the client, confirm tool availability and set printer/material/calibration parameters.

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Specify a small measured part with named dimensions and printer/material context.
  2. Review generated code, six views, section cuts and print checks; adjust dimensions deliberately.
  3. Export a small fit coupon and inspect it physically before committing to the full part and slicer settings.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
First use downloads a few hundred MB; minimum CPU, RAM, VRAM and full disk space are not documented. Physical calibration needs the user’s actual FDM printer/material.
Software
uv-managed Python/package dependencies, build123d/OpenCascade and an MCP client that passes images to the selected model. A slicer/printer is separate from digital authoring.
Platforms
CI covers macOS, Ubuntu and Windows according to the README. Native Windows lacks an OS sandbox; Docker/WSL2 is the stronger isolation route. SSH server mode is macOS/Linux only.

How it uses AI

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.

First project

  3. Compare original/edited 3D and export the PLY. Apply all-around fill before a replacement; current edits do not stack arbitrarily.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
64 GB RAM recommended; only NVIDIA 32 GB VRAM (RTX 5090) tested. Product weights about 27 GB, replacement adds 3.3 GB, optional orbit adds about 43 GB; all-around output roughly 1.4–2.1 GB/run. Smaller GPUs remain untested.
Software
ComfyUI 0.37+ with core nodes, author-tested 0.41.0; gsplat 1.5+ matching torch/CUDA and possibly nvcc/C++ compiler. Author environment: Python 3.12.3, torch 2.9.1+cu130, Ubuntu 24.04.5, driver 580/CUDA 13.
Platforms
Only one Ubuntu/RTX 5090 environment was tested by the author. Windows instructions are untested starting points; Mac inference support is not established.

  • Weights: AGPL application grant; SAM, Qwen-Image 2.1 research/non-commercial weights, MoGe, TripoSplat/DINOv3 and scene datasets have separate terms. Open weights does not give unrestricted output rights.
  • Commercial: Software use follows AGPL-3.0. AGPL application grant; SAM, Qwen-Image 2.1 research/non-commercial weights, MoGe, TripoSplat/DINOv3 and scene datasets have separate terms. Open weights does not give unrestricted output rights.
  • Cost: AGPL app/local GPU compute. Model and scene rights, especially Qwen research terms, constrain use independently.

How it uses AI

How it uses AI

A ComfyUI app combines photo-conditioned miniature image generation, a fixed Blender camera guide and LTX video to turn a tiny diorama into a real-photo reveal. [1]

Introduction

A ComfyUI app combines photo-conditioned miniature image generation, a fixed Blender camera guide and LTX video to turn a tiny diorama into a real-photo reveal. [1]

What it is good for

Template-based personal, product and venue mini-world clips with a consistent camera shape and a deliberately selected photographic reveal. [1]

Demo & examples

The README links selfie, shop, desk and car previews. The author reports an M3 Ultra run; its 50–95 minute examples are not universal timing guarantees. [1]

Install

Prepare ComfyUI 0.42+ with App Mode/frontend prerequisites, LTXVideo nodes, FFmpeg and the separately licensed weights. Clone the tool into custom_nodes, install dependencies in ComfyUI’s environment, restart and open its tiny-world graph in App Mode. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare ComfyUI 0.42+ with App Mode/frontend prerequisites, LTXVideo nodes, FFmpeg and the separately licensed weights. Clone the tool into custom_nodes, install dependencies in ComfyUI’s environment, restart and open its tiny-world graph in App Mode.
cd ComfyUI/custom_nodes
git clone https://github.com/Lightricks/ComfyUI-LTXVideo
git clone https://github.com/jk-automations/tiny-world tiny-world
pip install -r ComfyUI-LTXVideo/requirements.txt
pip install "opencv-python-headless<5"

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Select an appropriate template and provide your permitted selfie or location plus the required detail photos.
  2. Inspect the generated miniature and optional vision interpretation before committing to a long video run.
  3. Review the reveal, text and audio; change the seed or source selection when identity/layout drifts.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
About 150 GB model files. LTX bf16 transformer alone is about 39 GB; author describes 48 GB+ VRAM as comfortable and 64 GB+ system RAM offloading for smaller cards, untested on NVIDIA. The tested 512 GB Mac is not a minimum.
Software
ComfyUI 0.42+, frontend 1.41.13+; author fresh-install test used 0.43. FFmpeg, LTXVideo and compatible OpenCV are documented. Optional photo reading uses local Ollama vision or a separate cloud endpoint.
Platforms
Author-tested Mac Studio Apple M3 Ultra/512 GB. NVIDIA is explicitly untested; source wording about offloading is guidance rather than a smaller-card validation.

  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 code/workflows/templates/music; Inter OFL and each Qwen/LTX/ACE-Step model have separate terms. LTX community conditions are not replaced by the app license.

How it uses AI

Introduction

What it is good for

Planning shot geometry and camera movement before a generative video render, with editable staging, performer motion and reviewed first frames. [1]

node scripts/install-sample.mjs
cd app && npm run dev

Local browser application; cloud inference is author-tested. Self-hosted ComfyUI whole-chain execution is explicitly untested, not assumed equivalent.

How it uses AI

Introduction

What it is good for

Install

  2. Copy the complete skills/pixelorama-skills directory into the chosen client’s skills location. Separately install/configure Pixelorama, its compatible local MCP bridge and Node.js. Installing the skill alone does not install/register the editor or bridge.

git clone https://github.com/oniszczukthommy-wq/Osmanthus.git
cd Osmanthus/mv-studio
MV_DISABLE_MODELS=1 npm start

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Import your own permitted media into the standalone canvas at loopback:8792.
  2. Configure one optional provider and review lyrics/shot plans before generation.
  3. Listen to candidate music, edit the MV and inspect actual export; do not assume the full inherited music environment exists.
  4. The new homepage inspiration control asks DeepSeek for editable theme/arrangement suggestions without starting a song. Review real-time lyric alignment separately; first synchronization can take preparation time.

Author-tested GPU setup: RTX 4070 Ti SUPER 16 GB VRAM and 32 GB RAM. Default MOSS and FLUX packs download 10.97 GB and 12.45 GB, about 23.42 GB combined before runtimes/outputs. No universal minimum or additional physical-GPU validation is established.
Software
Studio Python 3.11+; Windows source/wheel checks and Windows/Ubuntu Python 3.11/3.13 CPU CI are author-documented. The observed GPU environment uses Python 3.13.12, PyTorch 2.14.1+cu130 and exact ComfyUI/OpenMOSS revisions. Node is a contributor tool, not a user runtime requirement.
Platforms
The physical generation reference is Windows/NVIDIA RTX 4070 Ti SUPER. Ubuntu studio CPU CI does not establish a Linux generation setup; a validated macOS generation path is not documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4]

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 application with MIT adapted-node notices and OFL Inter fonts. The pinned MOSS conversion and selected FLUX files have separate source/weight evidence; no blanket permission is inferred from a hosting repository or the app license. ComfyUI, plugins and optional Ollama/model terms remain separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 application with MIT adapted-node notices and OFL Inter fonts. The pinned MOSS conversion and selected FLUX files have separate source/weight evidence; no blanket permission is inferred from a hosting repository or the app license. ComfyUI, plugins and optional Ollama/model terms remain separate.
  • Cost: Local compute, downloads and storage. The reviewed default local model packs do not require a paid API; selected models and optional local assistant still have separate terms.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4]

Limitations

The current 0.1.0-rc.1 contest preview still lacks a complete fresh GPU-runtime lock/install validation. Twelve- and 24-GB recommendations are unmeasured, and experimental CharRef is outside the default packs. Moving encoding to RAM does not resolve a system-RAM shortage. [1] [3] [4]

How it uses AI

A Claude Code-driven editor compares a software recording with project documentation, drafts a cited storyboard, renders FFmpeg proxies and exports platform-specific short videos through explicit approval gates. [1]

Introduction

A Claude Code-driven editor compares a software recording with project documentation, drafts a cited storyboard, renders FFmpeg proxies and exports platform-specific short videos through explicit approval gates. [1]

What it is good for

Turning a permitted software-demo recording into reviewed LinkedIn, Reels and Facebook edits with editable timing and captions. [1]

Demo & examples

The README documents the pipeline, EDL structure and quality checks. A completed independently assessed demo export was not established in this review; automated checks do not prove compelling pacing or accurate claims. [1]

Install

Obtain the source, prepare Python 3.13+, uv, FFmpeg 6+/FFprobe and an authenticated Claude Code client. Install the optional OCR extra if needed, inspect doctor output and replace the author’s default brand/writing-skill settings before starting. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Obtain the source, prepare Python 3.13+, uv, FFmpeg 6+/FFprobe and an authenticated Claude Code client. Install the optional OCR extra if needed, inspect doctor output and replace the author’s default brand/writing-skill settings before starting.
uv sync --extra ocr
uv run ave doctor
uv run ave init --name "My Project" --video demo.mp4 --docs "docs/*.md" --platform linkedin --platform reels
uv run ave run

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Use a recording and documentation you are permitted to send to the selected agent, and edit project.yaml for your identity and writing workflow.
  2. Review docs-versus-video conflicts, storyboard and copy, then approve or revise the proposed plan.
  3. Inspect proxies, caption/safe-zone checks and change requests before approving final platform renders.

Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3.13+, uv, FFmpeg/FFprobe 6+, Claude Code login/subscription; OCR uses RapidOCR/ONNX Runtime with first-use model downloads. NVIDIA NVENC is optional, with libx264 CPU fallback.
Platforms
The example FFmpeg install uses an apt-based Linux path. A completed Windows/macOS support matrix is not documented; the language/tool prerequisites alone do not prove those platforms.

OpenDub

Documented dubbing application; optional engines have separate validation limits · AGPL-3.0-or-later · first-profile

First complete guide in this library; v1.0.5 was published October 27, not a launch today.

Docs checked 2026-10-30 · Release v1.0.5 (2026-10-27) · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

The app grant and creative workflow are supported; independent reliability and complete dependency clearance remain unresolved.

Scope: Timed, editable translation and neural voice dubbing of authorized short videos.

Evidence checked 2026-10-30; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: Reviewed the complete current AGPL v3 text, byte-for-byte identical to a preserved complete standard AGPL review, and read the complete project NOTICE applying version 3-or-later. The NOTICE separately discloses bundled FFmpeg/PyAV GPL components and source offers, permissive vendor components and OFL fonts. App-code openness does not cover model weights, voice rights or API services; OmniVoice weights are non-commercial and optional engines have their own terms. [2] [3]
  • Creative results · documented: The author supplies a bilingual demo and timing claims; these are not an independent accuracy or voice-fidelity evaluation. [1] [5]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Installer/source launch routes and first-use steps are documented; independent reproduction has not been established. [1] [4]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: Five recent release records include app v1.0.5 on October 27 and extension releases; the queried issue list was empty. These bounded observations do not establish sustained support or blocker resolution. [6] [7]
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand creative use was not established from the primary project, release and issue evidence reviewed. [1] [7]
  • Models, services and costs · documented: AGPL-3.0-or-later application; OmniVoice code is Apache-2.0 but its weights are CC-BY-NC. Chatterbox/CosyVoice/VoxCPM terms differ, and IndexTTS-2 permission remains unconfirmed. Hosted Higgs/ElevenLabs services and voice/input rights are separate. The default Higgs pipeline sends audio/text/voice references to the service even though it keeps the full video local. Free/local routes must be selected and checked separately. Browser jobs are limited to five minutes. VoxCPM2 and IndexTTS-2 paths are explicitly untested; no lip-sync animation is established. Translation, timing and voice fidelity need human review. [1] [3] [4]

Source review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run the app. The default Higgs pipeline sends audio/text/voice references to the service even though it keeps the full video local. Translation, timing and voice fidelity need human review.

How it uses AI

Neural speech recognition, translation and voice cloning generate a timed foreign-language dub with editable lines and subtitle export. [1] [4]

Introduction

Neural speech recognition, translation and voice cloning generate a timed foreign-language dub with editable lines and subtitle export. [1] [4]

What it is good for

Preparing reviewable multilingual dubs of authorized creator videos while retaining music, effects, editable lines and subtitle files. [1] [4]

Demo & examples

The publisher supplies an English-to-Simplified-Chinese demo and reports roughly four minutes for a 74-second clip on an M1 Max using rate-limited Higgs calls. These are author observations, not independently reproduced benchmarks. The current official site also offers a free stock-voice browser route on supported desktop/Android browsers; it does not clone the speaker, downloading a dub requires a free sign-in, and iPhone support remains a preview. [1] [4] [8]

Install

Use the Windows or Apple Silicon full installer, which bundles Python/FFmpeg and packages, or obtain the source with Python 3.12 and FFmpeg built with libass/rubberband. The first local voice choice downloads its model; configure the selected local engine or an authorized provider account. [1] [4]

  1. Download the matching official release, or prepare the documented source dependencies.
  2. For source use, run the repository launcher to create its environment; select and review a voice engine and any provider configuration.
git clone https://gitlab.com/open-dubapp/OpenDub opendub
cd opendub
./run.sh

First project

Begin with a short authorized clip and compare original and dub before a longer project. [1] [4]

  1. Choose spoken/target languages, replace-voice or voice-over mode, and the selected local or hosted engine.
  2. Run a short dub, review translated lines and timing, then drag or edit one line and re-dub it.
  3. Compare both versions and export MP4, WAV and source/target SRT files.

Hardware & software

The documented paths have different runtimes and privacy/cost characteristics. [1] [4]

Hardware
The first OmniVoice download is about 3.3 GB; Demucs and Whisper small source-path downloads are about 80 MB and 480 MB. System RAM, universal VRAM and total disk minima are not documented. VoxCPM2’s separate untested route specifies NVIDIA 8 GB+.
Software
Source: Python 3.12, FFmpeg with libass/rubberband and requirements including av<19. Voice engines use isolated environments because dependency pins conflict. Browser route needs WebCodecs.
Platforms
Official full installers target Windows and Apple Silicon macOS. Browser/extension routes are documented for capable browsers. A validated Linux or Intel-Mac installer is not established.

License, model weights & costs

Complete application grant and NOTICE reviewed; each bundled runtime, model and service keeps its own terms. [2] [3] [1] [4] [9] [8]

  • Weights: AGPL-3.0-or-later application; OmniVoice code is Apache-2.0 but its weights are CC-BY-NC. Chatterbox/CosyVoice/VoxCPM terms differ, and IndexTTS-2 permission remains unconfirmed. Hosted Higgs/ElevenLabs services and voice/input rights are separate. The publisher separately identifies GPL FFmpeg/x264/x265 components in particular desktop installers and offers their corresponding source; these do not become AGPL application code.
  • Commercial: AGPL app-code permissions do not make non-commercial OmniVoice weights or unconfirmed engines suitable for commercial outputs. Check the chosen engine, service and voice rights.
  • Cost: Local compute/model downloads for local engines, or separately charged provider calls. The publisher reports about 70 Higgs calls for its demonstration; no universal per-video price is documented. The official stock-voice browser route is free within its documented limits; it differs from the local cloned voice and paid BYOK routes.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented neural dubbing/editing workflow and reviewed software grant. Publisher demos/tests do not establish reliable independent quality. [1] [4]

Limitations

The default Higgs pipeline sends audio/text/voice references to the service even though it keeps the full video local. Free/local routes must be selected and checked separately. Browser jobs are limited to five minutes. VoxCPM2 and IndexTTS-2 paths are explicitly untested; no lip-sync animation is established. Translation, timing and voice fidelity need human review. [1] [4]

How it uses AI

A browser slide studio lets an optional LLM generate and revise editable TSX slide compositions, while local tools provide animation, narration, multilingual video and static deck export. [1] [4]

Introduction

A browser slide studio lets an optional LLM generate and revise editable TSX slide compositions, while local tools provide animation, narration, multilingual video and static deck export. [1] [4]

What it is good for

Editable animated teaching decks, design presentations and narrated slide videos with agent-assisted composition and local manual controls. [1] [4]

Demo & examples

The primary site presents a 4:18 fictional solar-company keynote with narration and local video rendering. It is an author example; text fit and visual polish were not independently reproduced. [1] [4]

Install

Use the official browser studio without an account for manual editing, or obtain the GitLab source and install its npm workspace. The source route serves the Studio at localhost:5320. Configure a model only for AI functions; the separate Rust/WASM updater follows its own build route. [1] [4]

  1. Obtain the official source or open the hosted Studio.
  2. Install npm dependencies and start dev:studio; configure optional model/voice access privately.
git clone https://gitlab.com/open-slides/openslides.git
cd openslides
npm install
npm run dev:studio

First project

Start from notes or the sample deck; keep generated content editable and review its export. [1] [4]

  1. Review a brief/outline and choose a visual direction; with no AI key the initial outline uses headings and bullets.
  2. Use the agent or manual controls to revise a slide; inspect code, overflow, fonts and actual provider costs.
  3. Record/generate narration and preview timing. Export a short video or static PDF/PPTX and compare it with the Studio view.

Hardware & software

Browser Studio and Rust/WASM updater are separate routes with different prerequisites. [1] [4]

Hardware
Minimum CPU, RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed README. Rendering requirements depend on deck complexity and chosen local models.
Software
Browser for hosted editing; Node/npm for source Studio. Optional openslides-render uses Hyperframes or Playwright. Rust/WASM is part of the separate updater source build; exact minimum versions are not established here.
Platforms
Browser workflow with phone/tablet editing described on the primary site. No independent OS/browser compatibility or mobile local-inference matrix was verified.

License, model weights & costs

Complete root dual-license grants reviewed; fonts, models and services retain separate terms. [2] [3] [1] [4]

  • Weights: No universal bundled LLM. Bring a separately licensed local model or a cloud account/key/credits; local/browser/cloud voice routes differ. Fonts have SIL OFL 1.1 terms, distinct from MIT OR Apache-2.0 code.
  • Commercial: MIT OR Apache-2.0 permits commercial code use under the selected grant’s conditions; no blanket font/model/media/service rights are implied.
  • Cost: Manual Studio is described as free without account; optional BYOK models, local compute and paid credits/voices remain separate.

Why it merits attention

Included for a concrete AI-assisted creative workflow. Author tests and polished demos do not replace independent use. [1] [4]

Limitations

Cloud AI and cloud voices send the relevant content to the chosen provider; local editing does not make every optional model private. Static PPTX export does not preserve all animated/interactive behavior, and author layout-fit measurements are not independent guarantees. [1] [4]

Dub Assistant — neural performance mixer
GPL-3.0		Exploring learned macro control for a live dub mix, rather than generating a finished song.
A Max for Live device learns a three-input, 24-output neural mapping for coordinated sends, probabilities and fader changes across eight tracks.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The complete GPL-3.0 grant includes an or-later application notice. Ableton Live 11/12 with Max for Live is a separate proprietary host; Windows and Intel/Apple Silicon macOS are author-documented. The checked device folder contains the AMXD, Max patch, JavaScript engine and sample data. Documentation is inconsistent: README describes 16 hidden neurons and the guide says 50 epochs, while current JavaScript uses eight hidden tanh units and 550 epochs, with at least eight training rows. Training/import behavior, audio-thread performance and actual mix results need verification. No audio demo, RAM/VRAM minimum or independent performance test was established. osc-bridge agent skill
GPL-3.0		Agent-assisted performance control with inspectable OSC routes and hardware-specific parameter mappings.
An AI-agent skill directs an OSC/MIDI bridge to operate synthesizers, DAWs and live-coding environments, including TouchDesigner and Pure Data.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The complete skill license is GPL-3.0; the current engine instead has a complete AGPL-3.0-or-later grant, despite the skill README saying their grants match. The skill describes an older cloned-catalog route while the current engine can fetch/cache SynthHub drivers; these setup versions need reconciliation. Individual device data retain separate source licenses, and proprietary DAWs, controllers and any model/client or hosted service have separate requirements and costs. A successful UDP send does not acknowledge host action; get_status may report no reply even with a running bridge, ports can collide, and OSC integer/float types and host-side plugins matter. Neither the stated 849-device catalog nor each cross-platform performance path was independently qualified.
